Rome → Charles V · nomenclator of three-letter groups and a homophonic alphabet, inline with clear Spanish · 18 April 1524

The Duke of Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 18 April 1524

Go to the full text ↓

Real Academia de la Historia, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 128–131 · DECODE R9877 and R9878, catalogued as Luis Fernández, Rome, April 1424, Non-decrypted · the imperial ambassador reports to the Emperor on the Swiss, the Bishop of Veroli’s letters and a request to the Pope for money

Chosen because the catalogue date, 1424, would have made it the longest ciphertext before 1450; the letter is dated 1524 on its own last page. The key was recovered from adjacent plaintext: Sessa’s sibling letters in the same volume carry the court’s decipherments. The nine cipher lines are deciphered word by word except two code groups and one spelled clause; two longer despatches, of 14 April 1524 and 24 February 1525, were then decoded with the same key. A check on 22 October 2026 found an open group or a non-word in most of their sentences; written out as continuous Spanish on 23 October, the letter of 14 April 1524 now reads, while that of 24 February 1525 stays decoded, not read. No earlier decipherment of the 18 April letter was found; Bergenroth did not calendar it.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 18 September 2026 · updated 24 November 2026

Method: key from adjacent plaintext · Extent: partial (46% of cipher tokens valued, not the word count) · On the map

With Brice Dudout (solution) and Prabal Gupta (transcription, key corrected) · Also credited · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used) · Gustav Adolph Bergenroth (key or decipherment used)

adjacent plaintext

At a glanceKey from adjacent plaintext · partial (46% of cipher tokens valued, not the word count)

The letters
The Duke of Sessa, imperial ambassador at Rome, to Charles V: the despatch of 18 April 1524 and its duplicate, and since 2 October 2026 two more of his despatches, 14 April 1524 and 24 February 1525, the day of Pavia. Since 20 November 2026 the page also holds a contributed decode of 30 records of the same correspondence, April 1524 to June 1525 (section 07); none of the 30 builds sentences yet.
Where
Real Academia de la Historia, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 128–131 (DECODE R9877, R9878, catalogued as “Luis Fernández, April 1424”), A-31 ff. 79–86 (R9873) and A-34 ff. 150–156 (R9898). The contributed decode covers 26 further DECODE records of A-31 and A-34 (section 07).
The cipher
One nomenclator for all of Sessa’s letters of 1523–25: three-letter code groups for words and syllables, single signs to spell, inline with clear Spanish.
How it was deciphered
The key was rebuilt from sibling letters that carry the court’s decipherment, over seventeen passes. Pass 16 used six marginal decipherments found on R9898, which had been thought to have none, and Bergenroth’s abstracts of R9873 as a check on the order of topics; pass 17 pooled the open groups of three letters and took tentative values that read in every occurrence.
What it says
On 18 April, the Swiss and a request to the Pope for money. On 14 April, the disbanding of the army, the Archbishop of Capua’s truce articles, a Pope who “ha estado en grandissimo conflito”. On 24 February 1525, the Datary’s French partisanship and Albany’s march on Naples: dueleme grandemente hallarme a lo que por razon y no por caso temo que suceda.
Measured
Tokens read as sense: f. 128, 104 of 116; R9873, 2,295 of 2,474 (92.8%; 87.8% if the pass-17 tentative values are counted as open); R9898, 2,441 of 3,059 (79.8%; 77.9% strict). Both stay partial, under 95%. These are tokens given a value, not reading figures: as text, R9873 builds sentences (23 October 2026) but reads at 88.3% of its words (measured 24 October 2026), under the 95% a complete reading needs, and R9898 does not build them.
Still open
On f. 128, vo, per and one spelled clause. In the two new letters, about 240 code groups seen once, with no deciphered occurrence anywhere, and on R9898 the bleed-through pages, where the cursive r, u and n cannot be told apart. In the contributed decode, 3,407 cipher units have no value, and its 4,910 ? positions have not been checked against the images.
Credit
Brice Dudout extended the key from cipher and clear pairs of the time (key KS4) and deciphered BNE MSS/20214/52-7 in part. Prabal Gupta (prabal-rje) transcribed 30 DECODE records with AI models, added 63 values to the key and decoded the records (pull request #826). The images are the Real Academia de la Historia’s, served through DECODE.

01The documents and their date

DECODE uploaded the Salazar volumes 9/26 to 9/39 in January 2026 as about a hundred records of Sessa and Lope Hurtado de Mendoza, in Rome from 1522 to 1526. Eight records in 9/31 carry the year 1424 (R9877 to R9884); the next ones, from the same volume and the same hands, carry 1524. The target leaf says so itself, on f. 128v: De Roma xviij de Abril 1524. The volume is the one Bergenroth cites as “M. Re. Ac. d. Hist. Salazar. A. 31” in the Calendar of State Papers, Spain, vol. 2.

BergenrothDateLetterSalazar A-31DECODE
63714 Apr 1524Sessa → Emperor, “deciphered by Don Manuel de Goicoechea”f. 79R9873, no decipherment bound
63917 Apr 1524Sessa → Emperorff. 138–140R9881, cipher f. 138, clear f. 140
—18 Apr 1524Sessa → Emperor, and the duplicateff. 128–131R9877, R9878
64322 Apr 1524Sessa → Emperorff. 163–173R9883, R9884, clear ff. 172–173
65119 May 1524Sessa → Emperorff. 320–325R9890, clear ff. 324–325

The two copies of 18 April were written from one draft. Every cipher token recurs on f. 130 in the same order, so the duplicate gives a second image but no variant spellings. Bound as f. 131 is the Italian letter of 6 April from the imperial postmaster, tutti Elvetij son in arme … expedisca volando a Milano et Roma, which Sessa cites.

02The cipher

A nomenclator in a rounded chancery hand. Three-letter groups stand for words and syllables: gap que, sof de, mus la, dim se, coh Su Santidad, fad rey, Jep paz, bob tregua, cum suyços, po3 Francia, mef Inglaterra. Single signs spell: α a, 4 o, x e or i, ꝺ r, nf p, ott t, # g, tto z, and a p with a loop is the plural -s. A flourished f marks each change between clear and cipher, and v db opens each ciphered paragraph as a null.

GroupValueWhere it was attested
raddichaJanuary 1525, la dicha letra
lif 3mostrar24 July 1524, ni se quiere mostrar
faquiere24 July 1524
z y-on8 September 1523, dilataron, concedieron
hudplática22 April 1524, January 1525, 24 July 1524
zumhay24 July 1524, ni hay memoria
boy, bez-idoJanuary 1525, perdido

The full table, with the passage behind each value, is in the repository’s key_working.md.

03The letter of 18 April 1524

The letter opens in clear. Sessa had seen a letter of the Bishop of Veroli from Constance and sent to the Pope for particulars. Felice, the papal secretary, told him the bishop’s letters of the 6th and 7th reported that the Swiss diet was over and that Lucerne had not agreed to help the French. Bern, Fribourg and Solothurn, the letter goes on, were spreading word that the Swiss in Lombardy were surrounded, so as to raise eight thousand men. The bishop’s friends in Switzerland wanted him to urge the Pope towards some form of peace. Esto es lo que me ha referido.

Then nine lines of cipher. Code groups are resolved; a hyphen joins letters spelled sign by sign; brackets mark what is not deciphered; bold is clear text written between the cipher runs.

las dichas [—]s no le las mostraron, que le ponen sospecha que desea aver algo que refiera de lo susodicho, aunque no / me parece que concuerda mucho con lo primero y postrero que el dicho obispo dize al maestro de postas / hablé en la ora a Su Santidad a pedirle que haya provisión de [—]-r-a los casos de suyços, persuadiéndole los m-re que r-ido los que no m-r-e-van / pues tiene color para ello, aviendo començado la plática de la paz y tregua … entender lo que mas podré. Al visorrey escrivo en el mismo punto.

In English: The said [letters] they did not show him, for they make him suspect a wish to get something out of them that he might report of the aforesaid, although not; it seems to me that it agrees closely with the first and last of what the said bishop writes to the postmaster. I spoke at once to His Holiness to ask him to make provision [of money] for the Swiss business, persuading him … since he has a pretext for it, having begun the negotiation of the peace and truce … to learn what more I can. I write to the Viceroy at the same moment.

Bergenroth’s abstract of the 22 April letter (CSP Spain 2, no. 643) continues the story: the Pope and the Swiss disapprove of the disbanding of the army, and there is “no provision of money from His Holiness or the Florentines beyond what I wrote”.

04The letter of 14 April 1524 (R9873)

A holograph of eight leaves (A-31 ff. 79–86), clear with cipher runs on ff. 80v–84r and a postscript wholly in cipher on f. 86r, dated in clear A xiiij de Abril 1524. No decipherment is bound with it. Bergenroth calendars it as no. 637, “deciphered by Don Manuel de Goicoechea” for him in the nineteenth century; no. 636 is the triplicate of the same despatch with a contemporary deciphering, and no. 638 a court abstract. Those English abstracts were used for the order of topics only: no group value was taken from them without a second Spanish context.

The whole letter was transcribed on 2 October 2026 and read with the pass-16 key. 2,165 of 2,473 cipher tokens read as sense (87.5%), each assembled word scored on a Golden Age Spanish model; 256 tokens are in open code groups and 52 spelled letters make no word.

Pass 17 (3 October 2026) pooled the open groups of R9873, R9898 and R9660 (Sessa’s letter of 1523): 84 groups occur in two or more letters, and a value was taken only where it reads in every occurrence, for example cas = tiene, var = agora, rac = dize, pam = paga, xoc = socorr-, sur = parte, yer = Florencia. All are tentative: none has a deciphered occurrence. With them, 2,295 of 2,474 tokens read as sense (92.8%); counting them as open, 87.8%. About 100 groups (145 tokens) and 34 spelled letters remain without sense; the non-words were re-checked at full zoom and stand as written, apart from one sign (f. 83r, pudiesse).

Written out, 23 October 2026. The reading audit of 22 October 2026 set R9873 back to decoded, not read: the working file was a line-by-line decode with an open group or a non-word in most sentences. It was then written out as continuous Spanish in its 1524 spelling, every word aligned with the decode or the clear text (r9873_reading.md, English r9873_en.md), with further values pooled from R9898, R9660 and a new sibling, R9899 (A-34 ff. 194–195, March 1525), whose one marginal decipherment gives hog = querria. Adopted: gal embia-, hog querria, kaz -mente, and, from sense only and flagged, reb dezi-, dis servido, sur/luc parte, gom quatro and others. The sentences hold through ff. 80v–82v, 83v–84r and the postscript, and agree topic by topic with CSP Spain 2 no. 636; f. 83r ll. 19–25 still do not hold. Counting every sense-only value and every look-alike letter as a hole, the letter stays just over the reading bar, so it reads (grade B); the flagged values await the triplicate’s contemporary decipherment (Gayangos papers) or Kolosova’s key. R9898 was written out the same way (r9898_reading.md) but stays decoded, not read: about 140 code groups occur once, and its bleed-through pages do not resolve.

  • ff. 80v–81r. The disbanding of the army in Navarre was known in Rome; the Emperor is said to be holding cortes in Burgos, then in Aragon and Catalonia, and coming to Italy with the grant, which does not please this court. The Archbishop of Capua left Blois on the 7th with the truce articles. Sessa proposed two things: that the archbishop go from France to England first, para satisfazer a la autoridad del rey, and that the King of France pay the King of England his old pension for the length of the truce.
  • ff. 81v–82r. The 4,000 ducats the Lodi men took at the Stradella, sent by the Pope under another name; the Pope at first muy bravo, then cooled. He will write to the Cardinal of Cortona that his nephew Ippolito is to assist at the government of Florence, has made good the 4,000 ducats and sends 4,000 more; Su Sd. ha estado en grandissimo conflito, not willing to disdain the enemies; those around him keep him irresolute, si no Micer Agostino Foyeta (Foglietta).
  • ff. 82v–83v. Money found on exchange for the viceroy’s army; the nuncio’s letters of 26 March from England: the King has no heart for the war; Wolsey would hear of a truce if the Pope got the King of France to send someone debaxo de alguna onesta color; the English will not contribute in Italy por ninguna via. Advice on the Emperor’s standing with friends and enemies, his nephews’ marriages, Alberto Pio, Count of Carpi.
  • f. 86r, the postscript. The Pope and his ministers are grieved beyond measure at the disbanding; he will not declare himself en liga defensiva ni ofensiva, though there was never a time so fit para acabar de ronper las alas a franceses; what holds him back is fear.

The order and substance match Bergenroth’s no. 636 point for point (the cortes, Capua from Blois, the two proposals, the pension, the 4,000 ducats, the nuncio’s letters of 26 March, the English refusal, Madame Margaret). That agreement is the check on the reading.

05The letter of 24 February 1525 (R9898)

A long despatch in a secretary’s hand (A-34 ff. 150–156), dated in clear De Roma a xxiiij de hebrero 1525, the day of the battle of Pavia and so written before the news. It is not calendared in CSP Spain iii.1. An earlier pass here said it carried no decipherment despite DECODE’s status; that was wrong. Six cipher runs have a contemporary marginal decipherment in a second hand (ff. 150r, 152r, 153v, 154r, 154v three times, 156r), and they were the cribs of pass 16: v em cierto, li mi, le me, net halla, sud datario, yol aqui, kun duque, and the name Micer Agostino Foyeta.

2,361 of 3,059 cipher tokens read as sense (77.2%) after pass 16; 300 tokens are in open groups, and 398 spelled letters make no word, most of them on ff. 150v–151r and 155v, where bleed-through on the images hides the difference between the cursive r, u and n. With the pass-17 tentative values, 2,441 (79.8%), or 77.9% counting them as open; 237 tokens in about 140 groups and 381 letters on the bleed-through pages remain. Re-cut at 1.5 times, those pages do not resolve further, and no other image of A-34 could be found.

  • ff. 150r–152r. The Pope did not answer the Emperor’s letters of 15 November and 15 December; the Datary’s French partisanship que abiertamente se conoce; the French have tried the English ambassador; the Pope offered to secure his master’s pension during the truce. In the margin: aqui ninguna cosa nos comunican, sabiendo que las entendemos y conocemos; por conjeturas sabemos lo que ocurre.
  • ff. 152v–154r. Albany’s march on the Kingdom of Naples; lo de Inglaterra se va calentando; the Archbishop of Capua kept at Piacenza con color de la platica de la paz; in the margin, se de buena parte que el datario ha tentado de reduzir al duque a franceses.
  • f. 154v. Sessa sent to San Germano by the viceroy; in the margin, y cierto si mi obligacion no me forçara … y viendo la malissima providencia que ha avido, dueleme grandemente hallarme a lo que por razon y no por caso temo que suceda.
  • ff. 155v–156r. Pavia expects relief; attacking the French in their strong position would be a manifest disadvantage. Postscript: the Duke of Ferrara has lent the King of France another 50,000 ducats, and (margin) no le plaze mucho al papa, aunque no quiere confessar, es en su menosprecio y que franceses le ayan tomado en su protecion.

06How the key was rebuilt

No key for this cipher survives, and none is in print. What survives is Sessa’s correspondence, and in a number of his letters the court wrote the decipherment on a separate leaf marked A Claro, B Claro. Each of those gives cipher and clear side by side. The work went through eight of them.

  • Three letters of April and May 1524 gave the first 60 groups: f. 138 against f. 140 (17 April), ff. 168v–171 against ff. 172–173 (22 April), f. 322 against ff. 324–325 (19 May).
  • Every cipher line was cut into images of single tokens by a script that splits the line at gaps in the ink, and each token was transcribed and aligned from its image. That separated look-alike signs the eye had merged: two p-loops (plural -s and b), two three-shapes (r and i), two hooked signs (d and u).
  • Six more letters were fetched from DECODE: January and February 1525, 24 and 29 July 1524, and seven of 1523. The January 1525 letter gave dicha; 24 July gave mostrar, quiere and hay. The February 1525 letter was at first taken to carry no decipherment; on 2 October 2026 six marginal decipherments were found on it (section 05).
  • Template matching then searched every downloaded page, about 110, for the target’s groups still without a value, at five scales. The September 1523 letter gave -on, twice, in dilataron and concedieron.

07The contributed decode

On 20 November 2026 a decode of 30 DECODE records of this correspondence was added to the repository. It is the work of Prabal Gupta (prabal-rje, pull request #826) and covers Sessa’s letters from Rome between April 1524 and June 1525 in Salazar A-31 and A-34. Four of the records are the letters of sections 03 to 05: R9873, R9877, R9878 and R9898. The other 26 were not among this page’s documents: R9874 to R9876, R9879 to R9881, R9883 to R9887, R9889 to R9897, R9899 to R9901 and R9903 to R9905. Some of those had been used here before, among them the deciphered siblings behind the key (R9881, R9883, R9884, R9890, R9893 and R9897; see Sources) and R9895, which had been transcribed.

His folder holds a transcription of the DECODE images made with AI models (1,347 rows), a key table with the source of every value, and the decode of each record, row by row. The key is KS4, Brice Dudout’s extension of this page’s key, with two departures (qes = es, and ha alone for njn) and 63 added values: 35 from clear texts of the time, 27 from the readings of R9873, R9898 and f. 128 given on this page, and one from two copies of one dispatch. The 27 are only as sure as those readings.

All 30 records are decoded, not read. The decode has holes in most rows and no record builds sentences, so it adds nothing to the figures in the box above. Of 20,998 cipher units, 12,351 have a letter value of the key and no doubt mark, 760 are switch marks and openers, 2,200 are doubtful, 1,186 take an added value, 1,094 have only a value fitted on the clerks’ decipherments (shown in the decode, never counted) and 3,407 have none. The 9 units written f~, the switch itself, are counted as doubtful because the label ends in the doubt mark; counted as switch marks, the two figures are 769 and 2,191. The 68.1% given in the pull request counts the 12,351, the switch marks and openers and the 1,186: 14,297 of 20,998, or 14,306 by the other count. It is a share of units and says nothing about words. His own estimate in words is 9,640, of which 4,906 are sure with the added values and 4,734 are holes.

Checked here, in the review of the pull request on 20 November 2026: a decoder written independently from his key table gives the same 1,347 decode lines from his rows; the unit counts recount from the files; and the 453 key rows that he takes from this folder equal KS4, or are the switch and the opener of this page’s key, apart from the two departures. Not checked here: his rows against the images, and his tests of the added values (planted values, held-out splits, agreement with the clerks’ texts), which his check.md records without their scripts. The rows also mark 4,910 positions with ?: clear writing, interlinear writing, or writing that the transcription did not make out. Nobody here has compared those positions with the images, so some of them may be cipher.

By his comparison of the decodes, some records are copies of one dispatch: R9874 is the last leaf of R9873, R9875 is probably its duplicate and R9876 copies part of it; R9879 and R9880 copy the cipher of R9881; R9892 copies R9893. That comparison was not run again here. The rows, the key table, the decode of each record and his checks are in the repository folder targets/sessa1524/prabal/, and each decode is listed under Full texts below.

08What remains uncertain

Grades follow the repository’s convention: C taken from a letter whose clear text is known, M uncertain, I inferred from context.

ReadingGradeBasis
las dichas … no le las mostraronCrad, lif, z y each attested in a deciphered sibling
que le ponen sospecha que desea aver algoMspelled letter by letter; one hooked sign is doubtful
que refiera de lo susoIz taken as o, the first half of -on
hablé en la ora a Su Santidad a pedirle que haya provisiónCcode groups attested; ora spelled
para los casos de suyços, persuadiéndoleCcum suyços; persuadiendo spelled
la plática de la paz y treguaChud attested three times
de lo susodichoIrus/ruc = dicho from five contexts in R9873 and R9898 (del dicho duque, los dichos quatro mill ducados), paired with rad = dicha
aunque noMhay = aunque is tentative: four contexts in R9873, none of them deciphered. Brice Dudout’s check of 29 October 2026 against the period decipherment of R9873 (BNE MSS/20214/52-1) gives hay = porque, not aunque (his findings, not re-checked here)

Open:

  • vo, a noun after las dichas, presumably the bishop’s letters or their copies. No match in any page, and absent from R9873 and R9898.
  • per, before -ra los casos. No match in any page, and absent from R9873 and R9898.
  • ruc and hay, after de lo suso, were open until 2 October 2026; the two new letters give them five and four contexts, dicho and, tentatively, aunque (above). Goicoechea’s nineteenth-century decipherment of R9873, at the Academy and not imaged, would confirm hay; a period decipherment of R9873 survives as BNE MSS/20214/52-1 (found by Dudout, 29 October 2026), and against it our unbracketed words agree in 92.8% (1,353 of 1,458) and the words in [?] in 34%, with several corrected values (gul = quando not si, pam = falta-, xoc = ayuda); not re-checked here.
  • In R9873, after pass 17, about 100 code groups (145 tokens) seen once, and 34 spelled letters that do not make a word; in R9898, about 140 code groups (237 tokens), absent from every deciphered sibling and from the six margins, and 381 letters on the bleed-through pages. The pass-17 values (cas tiene, var agora, rac dize, pam/Jir paga, xoc socorr-, leg mucho, sur/luc parte, len/lon buen-, 3ar aca, yer Florencia (the period decipherment BNE MSS/20214/52-1 gives alli, per Dudout, 29 October 2026), gig pudie- there against quisie- here, nuf gente and about fifteen from one context) are all tentative (grade I or M). The code is not alphabetical (cad sobre, cas tiene, cap tem-), so no bracketing applies.
  • The clause los m-re que r-ido los que no m-r-e-van: every sign has a value, but the sequence is not yet Spanish, so one value in it is wrong.
  • In the contributed decode (section 07): 3,407 cipher units with no value, 1,094 with a clerk value only and 2,200 doubtful units, and 4,910 ? positions that have not been checked against the images.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

R9877 / R9878, Rome, 18 Apr 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 ff. 128-131)

The Duke of Sessa, imperial ambassador in Rome, to Charles V (DECODE's '1424' is a typo): the bishop of Veroli's report from Constance on the Swiss diet and cantons arming for France; in cipher, that the Pope's secretary Felice would not show the bishop's own letters, that the report agrees with what the bishop wrote to the postmaster, and that Sessa asked the Pope for a provision for the Swiss business, having opened the peace-and-truce negotiation. Key rebuilt from the sibling letters' contemporary decipherments.

Transcription

transcription_f128.txt

# Sessa -> Charles V, Rome 18 Apr 1524 (RAH 9/31 = Salazar A-31 f.128r-v; duplicate ff.130-131). Cipher passage, pass 1.
# Two copies collated (f.128 = R9877_P1 right page; f.130 = R9878_P1 right page). f~ = clear/cipher switch. ꝑo = plural-s sign.
# ꝺ and 3 are two r-like signs not yet told apart reliably; g and 3 similar; x and ⅄ similar.
[clear] ... exortase a sus. q buscase alguna buena forma de paz. esto es lo q me ha Referido α—
L0: f~ v db mus ꝑo rad ꝑo vo ꝑo ler le mus ꝑo lif 3 z y gap le nf 4 do x
L1: ꝑo Jim nf g ttt bo3 do gap sof ꝺ x α boe ꝺ an # 4 gap g3 oo Є x ꝺ o     (f.130: ...gap g3 oo Є x ꝺ o)
L2: Є x ꝺ α suf 4 cih ꝑo z rus hay lex f~ me parece q concuerda mucho
L3: con lo primero y postrero q el dicho obpo dize al maestro de postas
L4: f~ bla g qid mus 4 ꝺ α α coh α f~ pedirle q haya provisio f~ sof
L5: per ꝺ α kel ꝑo ttt α ꝑo Jim sof cum nf g ꝺ cih α g ꝺ qid ram
L6: kef ꝑo v ⅄g gap ꝺ boy kef ꝑo gap lex v ꝺ g bad do f~ pues tiene
L7: color para ello / aviendo comenzado f~ mus hud suf α Jep 4 bob ꝺ
L8 (f.128v): q α f~ entender lo q mas podre α— Al visorrey escrivo en el mismo punto / Sa.ma Ce. Ca. Mt. nro sor guarde la Sa.ma persona de V.M. con augmento de mayores Reynos e Senorios / De Roma xviij de Abril 1524

# ---- pass 2 (token sheets tok/f128_L*.png, lines located by ink profile; token numbers in brackets) ----
# Signs: ꝑo = plural -s (follows the group); ꝑ = y (the p-with-bar sign, = q+ of pass 1); Ɉ = d (hooked y-like sign, distinct from g); ꝺ = r (6-like); 3 = i; g = e; nf = p; 4 = o; α = a; ott = t; tto = b; ttt = c(a)?; cih = su.
P1: f~ v db | mus | ꝑo?rad | ꝑo?vo | ꝑo | lex | le | mus ꝑo | lif | ꝺ z y | gap | le | nf 4 | do x
P2: ꝑo | Jim | nf g | ttt | bo3 | do | gap | sof | ꝺ x | α | boe | ꝺ an | # 4 | gap | ꝺ 3 | oo | Є
P3: Є | x | ꝺ | α | suf 4 | cih | ꝑo | z | rus | hay | lex | f~ me parece q concuerda mucho
P4: con lo primero y postrero q el dicho obpo dize al maestro de postas
P5: f~ | b | la | g | qid | mus | 4 ꝺ α | α | coh | α | f~ pedirle q haya provisio f~ | sof
P6: per ꝺ α | kel ꝑo | ttt α ꝑo | Jim | sof | cum | nf g ꝺ | cih | α Ɉ | 3 | qid | ram
P7: kef ꝑo | ⅄ | g | gap | ꝺ | boy | kef ꝑo | gap | lex | v | ꝺ | g | bad | do | f~ pues tiene
P8: color para ello / aviendo comenzado f~ | mus | hud | suf α | lep | ꝑ | bob | ꝺ

# f. 128 (R9877, duplicate R9878): Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 18 Apr 1524, the reading

Copied 22 Oct 2026 verbatim from NOTES.md, section 3. The cipher transcription is `transcription_f128.txt`; the key `key_working.md`.

## 3. Reading of ff. 128–131 (2026-09-18, in part; eight passes, see key_working.md)

Clear opening (f. 128r): *Teniendo escripto y cerrado el pliego del duplicado de xiiij deste que sera con esta, vi
una letra del obispo Verulano que esta en Constancia, de que aqui embio copia. Luego embie a Su Santidad a
suplicalle me mandase avisar de alguna particularidad para dar noticia della do conviniese. Mandome a mi Felice su
secretario, el qual me ha dicho que las letras que tiene son del dicho Verulano de vj y vij del presente, do dize
que hera acabada la dieta, y que Lucerna no concluyo de dar ayuda a franceses; que los cantones de Berna y Friburk
y Solodor, y para mas facilitarla y traer los animos del pueblo, publican que los suyços en Lombardia estavan
cercados y en grand peligro, con que juntava hasta el numero de ocho mill, que davan nombre de partir muy presto,
pero que el no podia saber el quando, por estar en Costancia donde algunos de sus amigos de Suyça le escrivian para
que exortase a Sus. que buscase alguna buena forma de paz. Esto es lo que me ha referido.*

Cipher (eight lines on f. 128r, one on f. 128v; the duplicate ff. 130–131 is token-for-token the same). Method:
every line cut into tokens by `tokens.py` (sheets in `tok/`, one PNG per token in `tok/*_tiles/`), the same done
for the deciphered siblings f. 138, ff. 168v, 170–171 and 322, and values assigned by aligning those against
their clears (f. 140, f. 172, f. 324). Transcription: [transcription_f128.txt](transcription_f128.txt).

> ¶ las dichas [vo]s no le las mostraron, que le ponen sospecha que desea aver algo que r-e-f-i-e-r-a de lo suso
> [ruc] [hay] no / **me parece que concuerda mucho con lo primero y postrero que el dicho obispo dize al maestro de
> postas** / hablé en la ora a Su Santidad a **pedirle que haya provisión** de [pa]-r-a los casos de suyços,
> persuadiéndole los m-re que r-ido los que no m-r-e-van / **pues tiene color para ello, aviendo començado** la
> plática de la paz y tregua r-[…] **entender lo que mas podré. Al visorrey escrivo en el mismo punto.**

Sense: Felice will not show Sessa the bishop's own letters, since they make him suspect that Sessa wants to get
something out of them for himself; Sessa nonetheless finds the report consistent with what the bishop writes to
the postmaster (Gabriel de Tassis: his Italian letter of 6 April, *tutti Elvetij son in arme … expedisca volando a
Milano et Roma*, is bound as f. 131); he went at once to the Pope to ask for a provision of money for the Swiss
business, persuading him that he has the colour (pretext) for it, having opened the peace-and-truce negotiation.
This is the news Bergenroth abstracts from the 22 April letter (CSP 643).

Pass 10 (the January 1525 letter R9897, same cipher, verbose clear on ff. 14v–15; fetched with the saved DECODE
cookie) attested `rad` = *dicha*, `boy`/`bez` = *-ido*, `Є` = *h*, `kel` = `kef` (*los*), and `lif 3` = *quiere*
(key_working.md, pass 10). Unread now: `vo` (the noun after *dichas*: the bishop's letters or their copies),
`z y` (the verb after *quiere*, *mostrar* from the sense), `per`, `rus`, `hay`, and the clause *los m-re que r-ido
los que no m-r-e-van*, where one sign value (probably `v`) is still wrong. The other 1525 letter, R9898 (14 pp.),
was then read through as well (pass 12): it seemed to carry no decipherment (wrong: see section 5, it has six marginal ones), so it gave only
contexts (`z y` also after *si*; `ruc`/`rus` is a person who *ha respondido*; `hud` again in *la plática de la paz*).
Pass 13 (the 24 July 1524 letter R9893, cipher f. 483 with its clear on f. 485) corrected `lif` to *mostrar*
(*ni se quiere mostrar* = `lep dim fa lif 3 α`, `fa` = *quiere*) and gave `zum` = *hay*, `tas` *cosa*, `luh`
*mundo*, `qit` *estado*, `lic` *monsieur*, `gel` *qual*. Open now: `vo`, `z y` (after *mostrar*; also after
*si* in 1525; probably a conjunction, *porque* or *diziendo*), `per`, `rus`/`ruc` (a person), and the clause
*los m-re que r-ido*.

Pass 14 matched templates of the unread tokens against every downloaded page (`tmatch.py`). The September 1523
letter R9834, whose cipher faces its clear on f. 38, gives `z y` = *-on* (*dilataron*, *concedieron*), so the first
sentence reads *las dichas [vo]s no le las mostraron*. `ruc` and `hay` occur only in the 14 April 1524 letter R9873,
which has no decipherment; `vo` and `per` match nowhere. Those four are the residue.

The seven 1523 Sessa letters DECODE marks "Decrypted" (R9676, R9691, R9693, R9781, R9834, R9836, R9841; about 50
pages, fetched into `img/` with the cookie) use **the same cipher** (`sof`, `gap`, `ram`, `bas`, `qes`, `lex`, `cih`
all recur; R9781's August 1523 cipher block has its clear on f. 651). They are the remaining source for the four
open groups; aligning them is the same token-sheet work as passes 1–13. Pass 11 (the rest of
R9897, ff. 10–13v, against ff. 14v–15) confirmed `hud` = *plática*, `rad` = *dicha*, and added *larga*, *fue*,
*esto*, *tiempo*, *para*, *después*, *llegar*, *miento*, but none of the five (key_working.md, pass 11).
Reading

# Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 18 April 1524 (RAH Salazar, f. 128, R9877): reading

## Reading

Teniendo escripto y cerrado el pliego del duplicado de xiiij deste que sera con esta, vi una letra del obispo Verulano que esta en Constancia, de que aqui embio copia. Luego embie a Su Santidad a suplicalle me mandase avisar de alguna particularidad para dar noticia della do conviniese. Mandome a mi Felice su secretario, el qual me ha dicho que las letras que tiene son del dicho Verulano de vj y vij del presente, do dize que hera acabada la dieta, y que Lucerna no concluyo de dar ayuda a franceses; que los cantones de Berna y Friburk y Solodor, y para mas facilitarla y traer los animos del pueblo, publican que los suyços en Lombardia estavan cercados y en grand peligro, con que juntava hasta el numero de ocho mill, que davan nombre de partir muy presto, pero que el no podia saber el quando, por estar en Costancia donde algunos de sus amigos de Suyça le escrivian para que exortase a Sus. que buscase alguna buena forma de paz. Esto es lo que me ha referido.

Las dichas [?vo]s no le las mostraron, que le ponen sospecha que desea aver algo que refiera de lo suso […] […] no. Me parece que concuerda mucho con lo primero y postrero que el dicho obispo dize al maestro de postas. Hablé en la ora a Su Santidad a pedirle que haya provisión de [?pa]ra los casos de suyços, persuadiéndole los m-re que r-ido los que no m-r-e-van, pues tiene color para ello, aviendo començado la plática de la paz y tregua r-[…]. Entender lo que mas podré. Al visorrey escrivo en el mismo punto.

# Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 18 April 1524 (RAH Salazar, f. 128, R9877): English translation

Having written and closed the packet of the duplicate of the 14th of this month, which will come with this, I saw a
letter of the Bishop Verulano, who is at Constance, of which I send a copy herewith. I then sent to His Holiness to beg
him to have me told of any particular, so as to give notice of it where fitting. He sent me Felice, his secretary, who
has told me that the letters he has are from the said Verulano, of the 6th and 7th of this month, where he says that
the diet was over, and that Lucerne did not conclude to give help to the French; that the cantons of Bern, Fribourg and
Solothurn — and to make it easier and win over the minds of the people they publish that the Swiss in Lombardy were
surrounded and in great danger — with which he was gathering up to the number of eight thousand, who gave out that
they would leave very soon, but that he could not know when, being at Constance, where some of his friends from
Switzerland wrote to him to exhort His Holiness to seek some good form of peace. This is what he has reported to me.

The said [?vo]s they did not show him, for they make him suspect that [he] wishes to have something to report of the
above […] […] not. It seems to me that it agrees closely with the first and last things that the said bishop says to
the master of the posts. I spoke at once to His Holiness to ask him that there be provision [?for] the affairs of the
Swiss, persuading him los m-re que r-ido los que no m-r-e-van, since he has a pretext for it, having begun the talk of
peace and truce r-[…]. I shall learn what more I can. I am writing to the Viceroy on the same point.

R9873, Rome, 14 Apr 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 ff. 79-86) read in part

Sessa to Charles V, a holograph with cipher runs and a postscript wholly in cipher; no decipherment is bound with it (Bergenroth's CSP Spain ii 637 calendars Goicoechea's 19th-century reading, and nos. 636/638 the triplicate and the court abstract, used only as a topic crib). Transcribed in full; each line (a) cipher, (b) reading.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (88% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

read_r9873.md

# R9873: Duke of Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 14 April 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 ff. 79-86)

Status: read in part (2 Oct 2026; pass 17 3 Oct 2026). Transcription and reading line by line: [r9873_cipher.txt](r9873_cipher.txt).
Key: [key_working.md](key_working.md), passes 1-16. Measured with `python measure_read.py r9873_cipher.txt`.

## The document

DECODE R9873, 8 images (P1 = f. 86v address + f. 79r; P7 = ff. 79v-80r; P3 = 80v-81r; P5 = 81v-82r; P2 = 82v-83r;
P4 = 83v-84r; P8 = 84v-85r; P6 = 85v-86r). Holograph, clear with cipher runs on ff. 80v-84r and a postscript wholly in
cipher on f. 86r, dated in clear *A xiiij de Abril 1524*. ff. 79r-80r, 84v-85v are clear.

No decipherment is bound with it. Bergenroth (CSP Spain ii no. 637) calendars it as "deciphered by Don Manuel de
Goicoechea" (19th century) and "in all essential parts identical with" no. 636, the triplicate of the same despatch
(Gayangos MS, contemporary deciphering), and no. 638 is a court abstract of it with Gattinara's notes. Those three
abstracts ([csp/csp2_636_638.txt](csp/csp2_636_638.txt)) were used as a topic crib only: they paraphrase in English,
so no group value was taken from them without a second Spanish context.

## Measurement

| | tokens |
|---|---|
| cipher tokens in the (b) reading | 2,473 |
| read as sense, pass 16 | 2,165 (87.5%) |
| read as sense, pass 17 (pooled tentative values; key_working.md) | 2,295 of 2,474 (**92.8%**); strict, pass-17 values open: 87.8% |
| in open code groups | 256 |
| spelled letters that do not make a word (a sign value or a transcription slip) | 52 |
| tentative values counted as read | 69 groups |

`lm.best_language` on the assembled reading: es-modern -2.03, ca -2.35, pt -2.67.

## Content (cipher passages, in order)

- **f. 80v-81r.** The disbanding of the army in Navarre was already known in Rome; it is said there that the Emperor
  will hold cortes in Burgos and then in Aragon and Catalonia and, with the service granted, come to Italy, which
  does not please this court. The coming of the Archbishop of Capua (he left Blois on the 7th with the truce
  articles; the Pope had a copy given to Sessa) would settle *estas alteraciones*. Sessa proposed two things: that
  the archbishop go from France to England first, *para satisfazer a la autoridad del rey*, and that the King of
  France pay the King of England the pension he used to pay before the war, for the length of the truce. Starting
  from the Pope it would have been easier; it was not done, *no se si del poco aparejo que saben* there is for
  carrying the war on; they try to settle things with less difficult conditions. Couriers by sea and by land
  (Rodrigo de Mayorga); the coming, quality and nature of *el dicho duque*.
- **f. 81v.** The Emperor's estimation; the 4,000 ducats the Lodi men took at the Stradella, sent by the Pope *con
  nombre que yva al [kam] de su casa*.
- **f. 82r.** The Pope at first *muy bravo*, then cooled; his difficulty in contributing to the army; the Florentines
  have given nothing; he has told Sessa he will write this week to the Cardinal of Cortona that Ippolito, his nephew,
  is to assist at the government (of Florence); he has made good the said 4,000 ducats lost and is sending another
  4,000 this week; he is *en grandissimo conflicto*, temporising, not willing to disdain the enemies; nothing hoped
  for has come from England; those near him conform him in this irresolution and draw him away, *si no Micer
  Agostino Foyeta* (Foglietta); Sessa declares himself *mas aficionado* (to the Pope's service).
- **f. 82v.** Sessa has found a sum of ducats on exchange for the viceroy's need to keep the army (with the bishops
  of Salamanca and Ávila). England: the nuncio's letters of 26 March say the King takes no great heart to undertake
  the war and waits for the Emperor's resolution; the Cardinal (Wolsey) said lately that if the Pope got the King of
  France to send a person *debaxo de alguna onesta color*, the truce or peace would be heard, so as not to seem to
  incline to it himself, *que seria perder de reputacion*; the English will not contribute to the war in Italy *por
  ninguna via*; Madama (Margaret) writes the same.
- **f. 83r.** Advice: the Emperor's estimation, in peace or in war, must grow and be kept, more with friends than
  with enemies; the confederates; to treat with the Pope; marriages and the advancement of his nephews; the Emperor's
  coming to Italy, *sin hablarlo con Su Sd.*; *no fiandose V.Md. en la que hizo con la buena memoria del papa Leon*.
- **f. 83v.** If the Emperor came to Italy with great power ...; the pontificate; Alberto Pio, Count of Carpi *(que
  da al B-e-r-t-o del C-a-r-p-i-o)* and his credit with the Datary.
- **f. 86r (postscript).** The Pope and his ministers are grieved beyond measure at the disbanding; those who advised
  his irresolution think they have won; he will not declare himself in a defensive or offensive league, although they
  judge there was never a time so fit to break the wings of the French; if on that frontier and in Picardy something
  were done, the Pope would decide, though Sessa has always known him inclined to abase the French; what holds him
  back is fear; they wait for what the Archbishop of Capua brings.

The order and substance match CSP 636 point for point (cortes; Capua from Blois; the two proposals; the pension; ill
prepared for war; 4,000 ducats at Lodi; the nuncio's letters of 26 March; the English will not contribute; Madame
Margaret; friends and enemies). That agreement is the check on the reading.

## Key additions from this letter (pass 16)

`ruc`/`rus` = dicho (with `rad` = dicha), `hay` = aunque (tentative), `fed` = rey de Francia, `xir` = cardenal,
`lot` = nuncio, `pof` = exercito, `kuc` = liga, `tig` = contra, `cuq` = tengo, `yod`/`quf` = amigo/enemigo,
`gat` = esto (*estonces*), and the name *Micer Agostino Foyeta*. These close the f. 128 residue *de lo suso[rus]
[hay] no* = *de lo susodicho, aunque no*.

## Remaining gaps

- about 120 code groups open, most attested once (`xoc` x4; `qat`, `var` x3; `yeg`, `siz`, `som`, `col`, `yam`,
  `Jam`, `cox`, `lon`, `dis`, `leg`, `gig`, `caz`, `lez`, `gul` x2) - blocker: open-codes; a single context does not
  fix them and no sibling decipherment contains them (pass-14 template search; the R9898 marginal decipherments)
- 52 spelled-letter tokens that do not assemble into words (e.g. *a-t-a-r-a-r*, *b-r-i-l-i-a*, *p-u-e-r-e*) -
  blocker: open-codes; one sign value or a transcription slip in each; a token-tile check (tokens.py) would settle them
- the duplicate/triplicate with Goicoechea's reading is not imaged - blocker: needs-physical-access (RAH; the
  triplicate is in the Gayangos MS, CSP 636)

r9873_reading.md

# R9873 — the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 14 April 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 ff. 79-86), the reading

Rests on: the cipher transcription and line-by-line decode `r9873_cipher.txt` (pass 16, 2 Oct 2026; DECODE images
IMG_R9873_I46008_P1-P8), the key in `key_working.md` (passes 1-17) and the b23 re-read (23 Oct 2026,
`texts/r9873-r9898_reread_log.md`). No decipherment of this copy exists on the DECODE record (8 images, every one
opened 23 Oct 2026: clear text and cipher only); Goicoechea's 19th-century reading (CSP Spain ii 637) and the
triplicate with its contemporary deciphering (CSP 636, Gayangos MS) are not imaged anywhere found. Bergenroth's
English abstracts (CSP 636-638, `csp/csp2_636_638.txt`) were used as a topic check only, never as the source of a word.

The text runs in the order of the working file: the clear passages it quotes, and the cipher runs joined into
sentences, in the period spelling. Where the working file does not transcribe a clear passage, a line
"[clear text not transcribed]" stands for it.

Marks: `[?word]` partly carried (a tentative code value, or spelled letters that need one sign read as its look-alike
or one letter mended); `[word]` supplied, carried by nothing; `[…]` unread; `[…kam]` an open code group (the group
named after the ellipsis, so that it counts as a hole). Unbracketed words are carried by the figures with a code value
attested in a deciphered sibling or by the clear text. Values adopted in b23 (see the log): `gal` embia (R9898 f.150r
margin), `hog` querria (R9899 f.194v margin), `cer` teni, `lar` miento, `sur`/`luc` parte, `gul` si, `nuf` gente,
`qu` o, `tt` s (plural), `reb` dezi-, `dis` servido, `gom` quatro, `lo` mill, `ror` ducado, `kaz` mente; the sense-only ones among them are flagged
for review in the log.

## Reading

### f. 80v (image P3, left)

Donde ha sido necessario he dicho lo que V.M. me manda sobre el deshazerse el exercito por esas partes.

[clear text not transcribed]

### f. 81r (P3, right)

Se sabia ya por Francia, aunque con algunas adiciones, porque quieren verificar que V.Md. hazia cortes en Burgos y despues las ternia en Aragon y Cataluña, y con el [?servicio] que […sig]s se [?sabe] se venia a [?Italia], que no satisfaze en todos los oydos de esta corte.

La [?venida] del arçobispo de Capua assegurara destas alteraciones, del qual se sabe oy que partio a los siete deste de Blaes con los apuntamientos de tregua que alli avia significado, de que Su Sd. le ha mandado dar la copia.

[?Antes] que de aqui […sas]ission, propuse dos cosas: la una que desde Francia pasase en Ingalaterra, porque para satisfazer a la autoridad del rey le pareçia que convenia, y tambien porque juntamente con el podria yr persona con resolucion a V.M.

Y la […fum] [?aunque] en los partidos de la dicha tregua por el tiempo que durase [?obligasse] el rey de Francia la pension al de Inglaterra que le solia dar [?antes] de la guerra.

Que saliendo de Su Sd. hera harto mejor y fuera punto mas facil para acabarlo en la paz, aviendose començado por la dicha tregua.

No se hizo; no se si del poco aparejo que saben que en essas partes y en las de Ingalaterra hay para pasar la guerra adelante.

[…qat]s [?piensan] atajar las cosas con condiciones menos dificultosas.

He lo tornado a dezir a Su Sd., y dize que aun seremos a tiempo.

Muy particularmente avise a V.M. con dos correos por [?tierra] y con otro por la mar, y despues con Rodrigo de Mayorga, el qual ha estado en Genova xx dias por falta de pasaje, de la [?venida] del dicho duque y de su calidad y natura, y de todo lo demas que me parecio convenir a este articulo; quiera Dios que los despachos sean llegados en salvamento.

Tambien, viendo que lo de Lombardia comiença a yr en prosperidad, escrivi al [?visorrey] […siz] […sul] se […som] […tup]

### f. 81v (P5, left)

Do V.Md. fuesse avisado del estado de la guerra.

Y que se le […col]se, que se le [?…]se a el [?…] y a [?monsieur] de [?Beorren] el […ce3ꝍ] [?aca] estas materias, porque desde alla con la variedad de la dicha guerra no se podria hazer juyzio claro de lo que conviniese, y para tirarse ya donde mas satisfiziese a la estimacion de V.Md. y se […] […] de sus estados.

Mandarlo ha proveer segun viere mas convenir a su servicio.

[clear text not transcribed]

Tambien se avra dado noticia de como los dias pasados tomaron los de Lodi en la Stradela quatro mill ducados que el papa embiava para ayuda de la paga de la gente, con nonbre que yva al […kam] de su casa.

### f. 82r (P5, right)

[clear text not transcribed]

Dexare de importunar, valga lo que valiere.

Al principio estuvo Su Sd. muy bravo y […hoz]a […liz]a [?…]; despues se ha enfriado.

No he podido hazer en este articulo la demostracion que [?fuera], porque teniendose por amigo de V.Md. no ha […kag], como si del todo fuera contrario o neutral; mas no se ha dexado de intentar lo necessario.

La dificultad que hazia en el contribuir para el sostenimiento del exercito, tanto por si como por [?florentines], ya la he dicho abundantemente.

[?Florentines] despues de […yam]o no ha dado ninguna cosa.

Ha le [?dicho] Su Sd. que despachara esta semana al cardenal de Cortona que […kar]a Ypolito, de […la] su sobrino, assistir al govierno de [?Florencia], y que [?llamara] que den […].

Su Sd. ha [?suplido] los dichos quatro mill ducados que se perdieron, y […kuf] en […] con pensa de […yam]s embio otros […cio] […] y otros quatro mill embiara esta semana.

[?Tratase] lo que [?…] por lo que [?…] esta.

[?Aunque] con la ynposibilidad encubre lo que quiere, lo que tengo para mostrar es que Su Sd. ha estado en grandissimo conflito, [?teniendo] el [?exercito] de lo de [?Lombardia], y no ha querido ni dexarnos a nosotros ni tanpoco desdeñar a los enemigos, y assi va tenporizado.

[?Socorre] a […qat] lo que ya tengo dicho [?…] que por esas partes ni por las de Inglaterra no se haze ninguno de los […njh]s que se esperavan.

# R9873 — the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 14 April 1524: English translation

Translated 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5) from `r9873_reading.md`, paragraph by paragraph, with the same holes:
`[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread, `[…kam]` an open code group. Modern spelling and
punctuation; names as in the reading. "V.M." / "V.Md." = Your Majesty; "Su Sd." = His Holiness (Clement VII).

## Translation

### f. 80v

Where it was needed I have said what Your Majesty commands me about the disbanding of the army in those parts.

[clear text not transcribed]

### f. 81r

It was already known through France, though with some additions, because they want to make out that Your Majesty
was holding cortes in Burgos and would then hold them in Aragon and Catalonia, and with the [?grant] that […sig]s
[?it is known] he was coming to [?Italy], which does not please all the ears of this court.

The [?coming] of the Archbishop of Capua will settle these disturbances; it is known today that he left Blois on the
seventh of this month with the articles of truce that he had indicated there, of which His Holiness has ordered a copy
to be given to me.

[?Before] […sas]ission from here, I proposed two things: the first, that he should pass from France into England,
because it seemed to me fitting in order to satisfy the King's dignity, and also because together with him a person
with a resolution could go to Your Majesty.

And the […fum] [?although] in the clauses of the said truce, for the time it lasted, the King of France [?should
bind himself to pay] the King of England the pension he used to give him [?before] the war.

That if it came from His Holiness it would be much better, and an easier point for concluding it in peace, the said
truce having been begun.

It was not done; I do not know whether because of the little preparation they know there is in those parts and in
England for carrying the war forward.

[…qat]s [?think] to cut matters short with less difficult conditions.

I have gone back to tell His Holiness, and he says we shall still be in time.

I informed Your Majesty in great detail by two couriers by [?land] and one by sea, and afterwards by Rodrigo de
Mayorga, who has been in Genoa twenty days for lack of a passage, of the [?coming] of the said duke and of his
quality and nature, and of everything else that seemed to me fitting on this article; God grant the despatches have
arrived safely.

Also, seeing that the affair of Lombardy is beginning to go prosperously, I wrote to the [?viceroy] […siz] […sul]
[…som] […tup]

### f. 81v

[…] Your Majesty be informed of the state of the war.

And that he should be […col]ed, that [?…] to him [?…] and to [?Monsieur] de [?Beaurain] the […ce3ꝍ] [?here] these
matters, because from there, with the changes of the said war, no clear judgement could be made of what would be
fitting, and so as to settle where it would best satisfy the reputation of Your Majesty, and [? …] [? …] of his states.

He will order it to be provided as he sees best for his service.

You will also have had news of how, some days ago, the men of Lodi took at the Stradella four thousand ducats that the
Pope was sending to help pay the troops, under the name that it was going to the […kam] of his household.

### f. 82r

The [?Florentines], after […yam], have given nothing.

What [?…] is being [?treated], because of what [?…] this.

[?Although] with the impossibility he hides what he wants, what I have to show is that His Holiness has been in very
great conflict, [?holding] the [?army] of the affair of [?Lombardy], and has not wished either to let us go or to
disdain the enemies, and so he goes on temporising.

[?Help] […qat] what I have already said [?…], that in those parts and in England none of the […njh]s that were
expected is being done.

And those who are close to His Holiness [?rather] confirm the […oap] and the […cod] so as to [?draw] him into this

Let Your Majesty hold this as certain and settled, and I say it because I am the more devoted.

### f. 82v

And debtor to his [?service], that [?…] for going about […] [?…] and [?is wont] [?…] to those who, I know, in the

In this the bishops of Salamanca and Ávila have shown themselves very well, and it is right that Your Majesty should

[…kam] also answers me, which is of the same kind.

As the least vassal and servant Your Majesty has, most abundant in faith and

His Holiness: I do not think the people of the palace are sorry at his going, because he kept occupied for them some

### f. 86r: the postscript

they judge that there was never a time so fit to finish clipping the wings of the French and to break up the troops at
the […hot] of the […], not [knowing] whether the war is to be made.

They show great dejection.

I hold it for certain that if something were done on those frontiers and on those of [?Picardy], His Holiness would
needs make up his mind, [?although] I have always [?known] him inclined to [?humble] the French.

And what has kept him confused has been fear of the […] [?…] that [?…], in which […nuc] us […kor] that […gud].

Without doubt, seeing no more [?…] […hon] no [?help] for the upkeep of the Lombardy affair, and he will stand aside
[?…]; [?now] he has begun it, leaning on what he expects the Archbishop of Capua will bring.

It brings him to satisfy them in what Your Majesty commands to be […xoc]ed in this article.

14 April 1524.

R9898, Rome, 24 Feb 1525 (RAH Salazar A-34 ff. 150-156) decoded, not read

Sessa to Charles V, written on the day of Pavia, before the news: Italy, the Pope and the datary's attempt to bring the Duke over to the French; six runs carry a marginal decipherment of the time, the rest is read here with the key. Not calendared in CSP iii.1.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R9898: Duke of Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 24 February 1525 (RAH Salazar A-34 ff. 150-156)

Status: read in part (2 Oct 2026; pass 17 3 Oct 2026). Transcription and reading line by line: [r9898_cipher.txt](r9898_cipher.txt).
Key: [key_working.md](key_working.md), passes 1-16. Measured with `python measure_read.py r9898_cipher.txt`.

## The document

DECODE R9898, 7 images (P1 = f. 150r; P2 = 150v-151r; P5 = 151v-152r; P3 = 152v-153r; P4 = 153v-154r;
P6 = 154v-155r; P7 = 155v-156r). A long despatch in a secretary's cursive hand, clear and cipher interleaved, dated
in clear *De Roma a xxiiij de hebrero 1525*, the day of Pavia and so written before the news (the next letter, CSP
Spain iii.1 no. 23 of 26 Feb, congratulates the Emperor). It is not calendared in CSP iii.1 (checked on BHO by
Salazar A-34 folio). f. 155r is clear; heavy bleed-through on 150v-151r and 155v.

**Correction to pass 12 (NOTES):** the letter does carry a decipherment of the time, in part. Six cipher runs have a
marginal decipherment in a second hand: f. 150r (the opening reply), f. 152r (*que aqui ninguna cosa nos comunican
...*), f. 153v (*o de color del papa*), f. 154r (*se de buena parte que el datario ha tentado de reduzir al duque a
franceses ...*), f. 154v (three runs: *para que a la ...ra se pierda ... y cierto si mi obligacion no me forçara*;
*y viendo la malissima providencia que ha avido, dueleme grandemente hallarme ...*; *que tenemos toda Italia ...*)
and f. 156r (*no le plaze mucho al papa, aunque no quiere confessar, es en su menosprecio y que franceses le ayan
tomado en su protecion*). They were used as cribs (key_working.md pass 16); the rest of the cipher has none.

## Measurement

| | tokens |
|---|---|
| cipher tokens in the (b) reading | 3,059 |
| read as sense, pass 16 | 2,361 (77.2%) |
| read as sense, pass 17 (pooled tentative values) | 2,441 (**79.8%**); strict 77.9% |
| in open code groups | 300 |
| spelled letters that do not make a word | 398 |
| tentative values counted as read | 98 groups |

`lm.best_language` on the assembled reading: es-modern -2.28, ca -2.57, pt -2.86. The 398 letters without sense sit
mostly in the bleed-through pages (150v, 151r, 155v), where the cursive *r*/*u*/*n* signs (`ꝍ`, `Ɉ`, `b`) are not
told apart reliably; the reading there is a sign-level transcription, not a reading.

## Content (cipher passages, in order)

- **f. 150r.** Reply to the Emperor's letters of 15 Nov and 15 Dec: the Pope did not answer; he wants what was sent
  not seen there; it must not be let known to the Swiss what has been sent from the Emperor; from past works one can
  judge the government from here on (margin).
- **f. 150v-151r.** The Pope's arrangement with the King of France and the French, a copy of which he says he sent
  the Emperor; his accustomed neutrality; the Datary's French partisanship, *que abiertamente se conoce*; the
  French *han yntentado con el embaxador de Inglaterra* ...; the Pope told him he would assure him of the pension
  due during the truce if the Emperor or the King of France did not agree; *el dicho [embaxador] ha respondido muy
  bien*; the invasion of the Kingdom of Naples; *su fin principal ha consistido e consiste* (clear) in a run that is
  open (`hem`, `lin`); the dicha tregua o paz; the Datary and the French; *la experiencia le ha mostrado*; the
  Emperor's agents.
- **f. 151v-152r.** The French *han efetado y efetan*; the Emperor may be sure of the dissimulation used; they
  promise *los mismos partidos*; the viceroy; the ministers outside the Pope's circle; *aqui ninguna cosa nos
  comunican, sabiendo que las entendemos y conocemos; por conjeturas sabemos lo que ocurre* (margin).
- **f. 152v-153r.** The Pope's attitude to Albany's coming and the invasion of Naples; *lo de Inglaterra se va
  calentando*; the Datary and the money that did not reach the camp, *la grandissima necesidad que en el canpo se
  pudiese*; the Venetians and the Pope blame each other; the Archbishop of Capua at Piacenza, *con color de la
  platica de la paz*, to keep him away; *Micer Agostino Foyeta*; the Duke of Albany's reception (clear) and *su
  venida a la ynvasion del Reyno*; the King of France.
- **f. 153v-154r.** No provision made in the Kingdom (of Naples); the Duke of Albany's men are badly paid, horse
  short; *se de buena parte que el datario ha tentado de reduzir al duque a franceses* (margin) with money and
  promises from the King of France; the Datary approves; *Micer Agostino Foyeta*.
- **f. 154v.** Sessa ordered to San Germano by the viceroy *para que ... se pierda, que no para defensar lo; y
  cierto si mi obligacion no me forçara* ...; *y viendo la malissima providencia que ha avido, dueleme grandemente
  hallarme a lo que por razon y no por caso temo que suceda*; *que tenemos toda Italia ... y no partira* (margins).
- **f. 155v-156r.** Pavia expects relief; forcing the French in their strong position would be a manifest
  disadvantage; what results in Lombardy; *el datario*; the Emperor must either content himself with *la perdida*
  or ...; postscript (clear): the Duke of Ferrara has lent the King of France another 50,000 ducats and munitions,
  and (cipher, margin) *no le plaze mucho al papa, aunque no quiere confessar, es en su menosprecio y que franceses
  le ayan tomado en su protecion*.

## Key additions from this letter (pass 16)

From the margins: `v em` = cierto, `li` = mi, `Jac` = obliga-, `le` = me, `net` = halla (not *toma*), `nuh` =
grande, `kch` = mal (confirmed), `cap` = tem-, `lec` = -mos, `dug` = sabi-, `doh` = sabe-, `yol` = aqui, `sud` =
datario, `pand`/`Jur` = buena/parte, `kun` = duque, `nah` = govierno (tentative). From context: `ruc` = dicho,
`ges` = embaxador, `cex` = trata-, `lin` = necesidad, `tig` = contra (tentative values in key_working.md).

## Remaining gaps

- about 140 code groups open (`cas` x6; `bup`, `sur`, `pam`, `kal` x4-5; `pol`, `tuf`, `cor`, `var`, `Jir` x3; the
  rest once or twice) - blocker: open-codes; single contexts, absent from every deciphered sibling and from the six
  marginal decipherments
- 398 spelled-letter tokens without sense, mostly on ff. 150v-151r and 155v - blocker: illegible; bleed-through on
  the DECODE images makes the cursive r/u/n signs ambiguous; a better image (RAH) is needed
- the opening run on f. 150r is half lost under the margin decipherment and the gutter - blocker: illegible

# R9898 — the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 24 February 1525 (RAH Salazar A-34 ff. 150-156), partial reading

Rests on: the cipher transcription and line-by-line decode `r9898_cipher.txt` (pass 16, 2 Oct 2026; DECODE images
IMG_R9898_I46118_P1-P7), the key in `key_working.md` (passes 1-17) and the b23 re-read (23 Oct 2026,
`texts/r9873-r9898_reread_log.md`). Six cipher runs carry a contemporary marginal decipherment in a second hand
(ff. 150r, 152r, 153v, 154r, 154v x3, 156r); there the reading follows the margin where the figures agree with it.
The DECODE record (7 images, re-fetched 23 Oct 2026) has nothing else: no attachment, no comment. Not calendared in
CSP Spain iii.1. The letter is in a cursive secretary hand with heavy bleed-through (ff. 150v-151r, 155v), where the
sign transcription itself is unsure.

The text runs in the order of the working file: the clear passages it quotes and the cipher runs joined into
sentences, in the period spelling. "[clear text not transcribed]" stands for a clear passage the working file leaves
out.

Marks: `[?word]` partly carried (a tentative value, or spelled letters that need one sign read as its look-alike or one
letter mended); `[word]` supplied, carried by nothing; `[…]` unread (here also every run of spelled letters that does
not make a word); `[…kam]` an open code group. **This is a partial reading: the document stays decoded, not read.**

BNE MSS/20214/52-7, Rome, 24 Jul 1524 (Brice Dudout) read in part

Sessa reports the Pope's words: Italy confused since the viceroy left; the Pope pressed to join a league that England wants, which he has not and will not consent to; news by way of Bourbon; the French suspicious and fearful; the bishop of Bayeux now an avowed French ambassador; Casale to England; Alberto Pio back from Viterbo; the confirmation of the league; Pescara takes Saluzzo, the army unpaid. Key KS4 (your key + five BNE cipher/clear pairs and R9893) validated on the held-out pair 52-6; 630 of 670 cipher words read (94.0%).

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (93% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# Lines = manuscript lines, V<view>[g/d = left/right page]L<n>. Text between « » = plain text written in the letter.
# Conventions: f~ omitted; "v db" (opening mark) kept; ꝛo written ꝑo; ? = illegible.
# The r-like sign has three forms, classified on the images before decoding: ꝺ = form A (bar + hanging 6, r),
# δ = form B (hooked loop, r), ϑ = form C (closed loop 8, = u, the key's `b`). A 73, B 1, C 26.

of this file per run of the letter (V0, V1, V2 = views). Notation:

- plain text = certain (KS4 groups agree with the word after the KS4 normalisation v→u, z→c, doubled letters reduced);
  text between « » is plain text in the letter (certain);
- {x} = probable (group outside KS4, or a doubtful sign; value from the sense and another occurrence);
- [x] = restored (letters missing from the cipher, never against certain letters);
- ‹g› = unresolved (the cipher groups are quoted).

(`bne52-7_count.txt`). Under 95 %: read in part.

V2 y darles este tiempo para ‹gab›r se y ‹ꝑ ꝑ do Ɉ 3 do α ꝺ α› otros franceses peligroso ; y si a la grandeza de V. Md. no bastan a obstar, bastara a ynpedir

V2 otros ‹disisos› o a de tardarlos y que sean mas costosos y dif{i}cultosos ; V. Md. como prudentisimo ordenara lo que fuere mas {servi}‹dis›

V2 «De su venida en Ytalia se habla muy mucho» y el papa muestra dessearla «quando lo de aca estuviese mejor asentado de lo que oy esta. Plega a dios que sea para la felicidad grande que los subditos y criados de V. M. deseamos»

V2 su Santidad «me dixo ayer de una letra que tenia» de Leon de Francia «y casi toda la sustancia de lo que contenia. Despues la hize y la hize copiar y la embio con esta a V. M. la qual le suplico que» no vea el nuncio «porque seria causa que no oviese otras. Bien creo que se la embiara» a ella

V2 «pero aun no hera arribado» Sigismundo «quando aquella provision hizo la madre del Rey ni tornado aqui alberto del Carpio ni hera» llegado a Inglaterra el cavallero Casal ni comencado a platicar en Venecia el obispo Bayus «que todo esto ha sydo casi una sazon»

V2 «El marques de Pescara avia tomado todo el marquesado de Saluzo y el marques se retiro en Francia con cierta infanteria que tenia y monsieur de Borbon tornava en Milan. Dizen me que passan grandissimo trabajo» en sostener la gente sin paga

bne52-7_en.md

# BNE MSS/20214/52-7 — Duke of Sessa to Charles V, Rome, 24 July 1524: English translation (working)

Translation of `bne52-7_reading.md` (Brice Dudout, 4 Nov 2026). It follows the Spanish closely; ‹…› marks the
unresolved cipher groups, [?] a word whose sense is not clear. Probable and restored words are translated without
marks (see the Spanish reading for their status).

The last letter I wrote to Your Majesty is of the 14th of this month, by a courier who I heard was being dispatched
from Naples, with whom I sent the duplicate of the 12th; and with this goes the duplicate of that one.

As to the troops which ‹rob›…, what the Pope answered me is certain, because someone published it on purpose.

What His Holiness has told me to write to Your Majesty is that everything in Italy is confused and everyone in the
greatest suspicion, which has arisen from the departure of the viceroy without leaving matters here settled; and that
His Holiness has been, and always is, much required and pressed by many to make a league, and in England they desire
it and are dying for new negotiations; that up to now His Holiness has not consented to it, nor will consent, and that
through his nuncio he has informed Your Majesty and given his opinion, which he would not give to a son ‹gi› more
truthfully nor with greater love; that, not being believed, nor ‹cor› in the degree he valued himself, he had done his
duty to God and to Your Majesty, and would wait for what the world would dispose. He complained here of the bad
treatment which ‹sol›, and so before ‹yem› ‹3m› ‹rep› [?] in the matters of benefices; and ‹rob›… that he knows the
viceroy left ill disposed in what concerns His Holiness, and that of the ‹kit› the ‹bie›s of ‹fu› are aggrieved.

He told me that they had been informed, by way of Monsieur de Bourbon, that the principal object he had given to Your
Majesty was to encourage him to make war on him. To me it seems ‹α # g› it should not ‹sof› be believed; but it is
certain that ‹qob›s fear it and hold it today as verified, and are more fearful and ‹3im› desperate, negotiating; and
in my judgement, if they do not find in France or in England what they hope for and keep for their remedy and
security, Your Majesty may take it as settled that they will avail themselves of the ‹boc›. This is what His Holiness
has told me, and he called me to write it to Your Majesty; and ‹3om› I have reported it in other letters of mine, I
did not think I should keep silent on it in this one, since he ordered it twice in the last three days.

The ‹bie›s raise as many enemies [as they can], the plots more; and here few are the days on which his ambassador is
not twice at the palace, and he dispatches couriers. What he negotiates cannot be discovered, because it goes between
His Holiness and the datary, nor does the archbishop of Capua reach it; ‹3om› suspects it and confesses it to me. So
they build great foundation on the discontent of England, of which there is open talk. The ambassador ‹3cp›, I think,
will have given Your Majesty particular account of what goes on there and of the very ordinary and ‹dup›s audiences
given to the bishop of Bayeux, who is now an avowed ambassador of France, and of the … of this court ‹boz› ‹# g›
‹mus› ‹ram› and ‹Jem› ‹α ka 3›.

From England, letters came the day before yesterday, of the 28th of last month, which are the freshest there are.
Joachino had arrived at that court. The Cardinal told the nuncio that he was asking a safe-conduct for the French
ambassadors and that it would be given; that he brings no other news; that there was no rumour of war; that they
already knew of the coming of the King of France to Your Majesty [sic], at which they were scandalised, and complained
that it had been done without their being told; in sum they showed themselves discontented. This is what I have been
able to learn. It is not ‹ser› to think that there is more; and if there is none now, there will be after the arrival
of the knight Casale, of whose going I have informed Your Majesty.

I have already written of the going of Sigismondo ‹dar› of the King of France, who resides here with his ambassador,
to Madame, to warn her of these plots.

Four days later Alberto da Carpi came, who had been at Viterbo for more than thirty days under colour of taking the
baths, but in truth because of the league made with Your Majesty, so as not to leave shamefully and to take from there
the road that suited him, since by the chapter on rebels he may not be in Rome nor in any other land of the Church. For
my part I hold that the new plots have turned him and that he does not sleep on them; and the datary ‹ka ϑ g x›
Monsieur ‹ϑ g x ges› has not yet come to the palace. I have not wished to tell it to His Holiness until I see what he
decides on the confirmation of the said [league]. I have given him the form which ‹sof kon›, as Your Majesty's
advocate advised, of which he sent me a copy. It is now eight days, and although I asked for an answer they do not
give it to me, as I said in others.

I know he will hold off until he sees what comes from ‹yac› and ‹3im› what begins here; nevertheless I will press to
get from them what he decides, and I will not fail to remind him. And I also beg Your Majesty that these matters
not ‹car› in suspense, and that you take the course that best suits your ‹dos› in them; and if it seems to you that
what was agreed does not satisfy, either let another be negotiated, or let these know that they are to be treated not
as friends, because they are suspicious and fearful; and to give them this time to ‹gab› themselves and ‹ꝑ ꝑ do Ɉ 3
do α ꝺ α› other Frenchmen is dangerous; and if they are not enough to withstand the greatness of Your Majesty, it will
be enough to hinder other ‹disisos›, or to delay them and make them more costly and difficult. Your Majesty, as most
prudent, will order what is most to your service.

Of your coming to Italy there is very much talk, and the Pope shows that he desires it, when matters here are better
settled than they are today. May it please God that it be for the great happiness that the subjects and servants of
Your Majesty desire.

His Holiness told me yesterday of a letter he had from Lyon in France, and almost all the substance of what it
contained. Afterwards I obtained it, had it copied, and send it with this to Your Majesty, whom I beg that the nuncio
not see it, because that would be the cause of there being no others. I well believe he will send it to her.

It does not seem that the going of the legate satisfies them at all, since she wishes to prevent it; but it is held
certain that it would not be in time, because it is understood that they had already passed; and had it not been so,
His Beatitude was resolved to revoke it and to resent it. This contradicts the plots that are believed to be treated
‹dup›s.

But Sigismondo had not yet arrived when the King's mother made that provision, nor had Alberto da Carpi returned here,
nor had the knight Casale reached England, nor had the bishop of Bayeux begun to negotiate in Venice: all this has
happened almost at one time.

The Marquis of Pescara had taken the whole marquisate of Saluzzo, and the marquis withdrew into France with some
infantry he had, and Monsieur de Bourbon was returning to Milan. They tell me they suffer very great hardship in
keeping the troops without pay.

R9873, Rome, 14 April 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 (9/31) ff. 79-86): prabal-rje's rows and decode decoded, not read

The French army is breaking up. The Pope will not declare himself in a league, defensive or offensive, and is thought to lean towards the French. Lombardy, the coming of the archbishop of Capua, Micer Agostino Foglietta, money for the army and the viceroy's need.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R9874: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, 14 April 1524 (last leaf of R9873)

- **DECODE:** record R9874, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9874.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 86-87.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9874_ff86-87_rows.txt`.
- **Read before:** The last leaf of the letter of 14 April 1524 (R9873), which the ledger read before me.
- **Units:** 492 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 339 with letters and 0 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 56. My added entries: 13 with letters and 0 nulls (2 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 4. No value: 80. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 33; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 1.
- **Words, estimated:** 196: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 83. Sure with my added values: 91. Holes: 105. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 56 of the 492 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key. This record is the last leaf of R9873.

## Summary

The last leaf of the letter of 14 April 1524 (R9873): the cipher postscript. Lombardy, the archbishop of Capua, help and money. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

R9875, Rome, not dated; probably the duplicate of 14 April 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 (9/31) ff. 88-95): prabal-rje's rows and decode decoded, not read

Much of its text recurs in the letter of 14 April 1524 (R9873), so it is probably the duplicate of that letter. Peace and truce, the King of France, England, Lombardy, the viceroy's need, Micer Agostino Foglietta, Alberto Pio da Carpi and the datary.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R9875: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, not dated; probably the duplicate of 14 April 1524

- **DECODE:** record R9875, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9875.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 88-95.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9875_ff88-95_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 2075 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 1159 with letters and 41 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 320. My added entries: 144 with letters and 13 nulls (23 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 72. No value: 326. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 511; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 44.
- **Words, estimated:** 904: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 379. Sure with my added values: 465. Holes: 439. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 320 of the 2075 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key. This record is probably the duplicate of R9873.

## Summary

Much of its text recurs in the letter of 14 April 1524 (R9873), so it is probably the duplicate of that letter. Peace and truce, the King of France, England, Lombardy, the viceroy's need, Micer Agostino Foglietta, Alberto Pio da Carpi and the datary. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

The marks are explained in [README.md](README.md#how-to-read-the-decode). Each line gives the record, the image page and the row of my rows file.

R9876, Rome, not dated; text shared with 14 April 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 (9/31) f. 96): prabal-rje's rows and decode decoded, not read

A copy of part of the letter of 14 April 1524. The Pope will not declare himself in a league, defensive or offensive. Sessa holds it for certain that, under pressure, the Pope would lean to the French out of fear. Little help, the affairs of Lombardy, and what the archbishop of Capua will bring.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription
