Decryption

r9876.md

# R9876: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, not dated; text shared with 14 April 1524

- **DECODE:** record R9876, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9876.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) f. 96.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9876_f96_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 550 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 315 with letters and 16 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 98. My added entries: 53 with letters and 7 nulls (28 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 4. No value: 57. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 16; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 1.
- **Words, estimated:** 137: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 44. Sure with my added values: 57. Holes: 80. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 98 of the 550 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key. This record is a copy of part of R9873.

## Summary

A copy of part of the letter of 14 April 1524. The Pope will not declare himself in a league, defensive or offensive. Sessa holds it for certain that, under pressure, the Pope would lean to the French out of fear. Little help, the affairs of Lombardy, and what the archbishop of Capua will bring. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

# R9880: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, April 1524; text shared with 17 April 1524

- **DECODE:** record R9880, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9880.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) f. 137.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9880_f137_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 258 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 154 with letters and 2 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 41. My added entries: 18 with letters and 9 nulls (4 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 2. No value: 32. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 41; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 1.
- **Words, estimated:** 116: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 64. Sure with my added values: 71. Holes: 45. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 41 of the 258 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key. This record is a copy of the cipher of R9881.

## Summary

The same cipher passage as R9881 (see there). This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

## The decode, row by row

The marks are explained in [README.md](README.md#how-to-read-the-decode). Each line gives the record, the image page and the row of my rows file.

- R9880, p. 1, C01: `· … he-l?-·-a?-t-l?-·-a? … r-do que el arçobispo de Capua no {parti}-o-de Francia muy con-t-l?-·-a?-t`
- R9880, p. 1, C02: `o-del?-a-{persona} del? da-l?-·-a?-ningun--a-{s} · {s} especialmente-l?-·-a?-{la} del?-a-ni-o-tregua-l?-·-a?`
- R9880, p. 1, C03: `que le {p} de-m-a-si-a-da · ·-t-o … que madama {la} ·-e-g-· ·-a?`
- R9880, p. 1, C04: `t-e-le {enbio} {despues} una {persona}-a-c-e-r-t-e-f?-r-c-a-r-le que · ·-h-·-r?-o-se`

R9881, Rome, 17 April 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 (9/31) ff. 139-140): prabal-rje's rows and decode decoded, not read

Close to the clerk's decipherment of the time. Sessa has understood that the archbishop of Capua did not leave France content, nor with any of the talks, above all that of peace or truce. Madame the Regent sent a person after him to assure him, so that he would go on with the enterprise; of the one and the other nothing is said. The Pope by no means wants to show displeasure with the French. Of England and of other parts nothing more is known than what Sessa has written.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R9881: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, 17 April 1524

- **DECODE:** record R9881, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9881.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 139-140.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9881_ff139-140_rows.txt`.
- **Read before:** The ledger read its cipher against the period decipherment f. 140 to build its key (key_working.md, passes 1, 3, 6 and 8).
- **Units:** 263 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 164 with letters and 9 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 39. My added entries: 8 with letters and 0 nulls (2 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 14. No value: 29. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 16; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 1.
- **Words, estimated:** 106: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 47. Sure with my added values: 51. Holes: 55. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 39 of the 263 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

Close to the clerk's decipherment of the time. Sessa has understood that the archbishop of Capua did not leave France content, nor with any of the talks, above all that of peace or truce. Madame the Regent sent a person after him to assure him, so that he would go on with the enterprise; of the one and the other nothing is said. The Pope by no means wants to show displeasure with the French. Of England and of other parts nothing more is known than what Sessa has written. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

Most of its rows are masked or do not read. The war will fall on the Pope's shoulders; the French show more contentment; the Cardinal of England, the archbishop of Capua, the Swiss. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

# R9884: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, 22 April 1524

- **DECODE:** record R9884, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9884.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 170-173.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9884_ff170-173_rows.txt`.
- **Read before:** The ledger read its cipher against the period decipherment f. 172 to build its key (key_working.md, passes 1 to 3).
- **Units:** 265 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 179 with letters and 0 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 31. My added entries: 14 with letters and 4 nulls (7 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 15. No value: 22. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 158; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 6.
- **Words, estimated:** 183: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 111. Sure with my added values: 118. Holes: 65. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 31 of the 265 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

Close to the clerk's decipherment of the time. All the war will fall on the Pope's shoulders. Neither the Pope nor the Florentines have made more provision of money than Sessa has written. The Cardinal of England and the Regent. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

# R9887: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, not dated (between 22 April and May 1524)

- **DECODE:** record R9887, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9887.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 274-277.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9887_ff274-277_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 581 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 326 with letters and 53 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 52. My added entries: 14 with letters and 2 nulls (4 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 19. No value: 115. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 60; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 5.
- **Words, estimated:** 294: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 145. Sure with my added values: 155. Holes: 139. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 52 of the 581 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

The Pope; it is impossible to secure; the enemy; Siena; someone in France was not very content; matters of Your Majesty. Several code values here are doubtful. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

# R9891: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, June 1524

- **DECODE:** record R9891, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9891.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 361-364.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9891_ff361-364_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 449 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 216 with letters and 1 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 57. My added entries: 29 with letters and 7 nulls (8 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 21. No value: 118. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 183; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 18.
- **Words, estimated:** 278: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 118. Sure with my added values: 138. Holes: 140. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 57 of the 449 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

The nuncio; the archbishop of Capua; a suspicion; help to someone in France; no money given; the datary; Alberto Pio da Carpi; the viceroy; the war. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

R9893, Rome, 24 July 1524 (RAH Salazar A-31 (9/31) ff. 483-485): prabal-rje's rows and decode decoded, not read

Close to the clerk's decipherment of the time. While he waits for Monsieur de la Roxa (as the clerk spells him), the Pope will not talk of anything that touches the state. There is no mention of peace, truce or war, as if he were not Pope. He will not show himself for one side or the other, so everything is in suspense. Sessa does not know what the Pope will do when that man comes, whom he awaits with desire.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription

R9893_ff483-485_rows.txt

# R9893: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, 24 July 1524

- **DECODE:** record R9893, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9893.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 483-485.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9893_ff483-485_rows.txt`.
- **Read before:** The ledger read its cipher against the clear leaf f. 485 (key_working.md, pass 13). Brice Dudout took values from it for KS4 (source C04).
- **Units:** 158 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 86 with letters and 10 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 21. My added entries: 11 with letters and 0 nulls (5 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 10. No value: 20. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 61; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 5.
- **Words, estimated:** 95: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 46. Sure with my added values: 51. Holes: 44. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 21 of the 158 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

Close to the clerk's decipherment of the time. While he waits for Monsieur de la Roxa (as the clerk spells him), the Pope will not talk of anything that touches the state. There is no mention of peace, truce or war, as if he were not Pope. He will not show himself for one side or the other, so everything is in suspense. Sessa does not know what the Pope will do when that man comes, whom he awaits with desire. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

r9896.md

# R9896: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, January 1525

- **DECODE:** record R9896, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9896.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-34 (9/34) ff. 6-9.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9896_ff6-9_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 1875 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 1027 with letters and 133 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 180. My added entries: 80 with letters and 6 nulls (60 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 250. No value: 199. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 253; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 31.
- **Words, estimated:** 707: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 293. Sure with my added values: 305. Holes: 402. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 180 of the 1875 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

The datary and the Pope; the Pope 'is here with the King of France'; people very warm with the datary; the nature of his master; Alberto Pio da Carpi; no provision from the viceroy; the need. Madame was content that the second son of the King of France should have an investiture. The suspicion that was taken; this article; after his letter came, no provision has been made. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

## The decode, row by row

The marks are explained in [README.md](README.md#how-to-read-the-decode). Each line gives the record, the image page and the row of my rows file.

Decryption

r9897.md

# R9897: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, 7 January 1525

- **DECODE:** record R9897, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9897.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-34 (9/34) ff. 10-16.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9897_ff10-16_rows.txt`.
- **Read before:** The ledger read its cipher against the clear of ff. 14v-15 to build its key (key_working.md, passes 10 and 11).
- **Units:** 1641 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 851 with letters and 92 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 200. My added entries: 77 with letters and 22 nulls (21 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 121. No value: 278. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 302; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 31.
- **Words, estimated:** 802: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 340. Sure with my added values: 380. Holes: 422. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 200 of the 1641 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

The said letter; no time has been lost; Parma; Your Majesty was content; at length; any preparation. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

R9898, Rome, 24 February 1525 (RAH Salazar A-34 (9/34) ff. 150-156): prabal-rje's rows and decode decoded, not read

The Pope and the datary, the datary's attempt to bring the Duke over to the French, money, the Swiss, and the government from here on.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R9899: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, March 1525

- **DECODE:** record R9899, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9899.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-34 (9/34) ff. 194-195.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9899_ff194-195_rows.txt`.
- **Read before:** The ledger read its one marginal decipherment (`hog` = querria; key_working.md, pass b23).
- **Units:** 218 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 106 with letters and 1 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 56. My added entries: 12 with letters and 0 nulls (1 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 6. No value: 37. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 51; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 2.
- **Words, estimated:** 119: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 47. Sure with my added values: 56. Holes: 63. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 56 of the 218 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

Too little is decoded to say. Time, place. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

Decryption

r9904.md

# R9904: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, not dated (June 1525?)

- **DECODE:** record R9904, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9904.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-34 (9/34) ff. 379-382.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9904_ff379-382_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 534 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 268 with letters and 1 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 64. My added entries: 26 with letters and 12 nulls (5 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 25. No value: 138. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 165; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 10.
- **Words, estimated:** 281: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 105. Sure with my added values: 120. Holes: 161. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 64 of the 534 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

It shares passages with R9905. The mother of the King of France sends an ambassador; the King of England; the Pope; whether they will come; resistance. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

Decryption

r9905.md

# R9905: the Duke of Sessa to Charles V, not dated (19 June 1525?)

- **DECODE:** record R9905, https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/9905.
- **Archive:** Real Academia de la Historia, Madrid, Colección Salazar y Castro A-34 (9/34) ff. 383-388.
- **State:** decoded, not read. The decode does not build sentences.
- **Key:** from key: the ledger's key (KS4 of Brice Dudout, on Daniel Bourdeau's `key_working.md`), with the values that I added (`key/added-values.tsv`). The full table of this decode is `key/key.tsv`.
- **Rows:** my own transcription of the DECODE images: `transcription/R9905_ff383-388_rows.txt`.
- **Units:** 826 cipher units, as I classify them. Ledger key, no doubt mark: 415 with letters and 53 switch marks or openers. Doubtful: 29. My added entries: 28 with letters and 16 nulls (12 of them carry my `~` doubt in the rows). Clerk value only: 82. No value: 203. `?` positions of my rows (`…`): 225; some may be cipher signs that I did not read. Clear words cut: 18.
- **Words, estimated:** 374: the tokens of the decode lines (an estimate; see README.md, "Words, estimated"). Sure by the ledger's key alone: 120. Sure with my added values: 129. Holes: 245. No read share, because the record is not read.
- **Doubt:** 29 of the 826 cipher units carry a doubt mark: a sign that I doubt in my rows, a sign map off its aligned page, or a clerk-copy value that differs from the key.

## Summary

It shares passages with R9904. The mother of the King of France sends an ambassador; the archbishop of Capua; the viceroy; money; resistance with the greater part. This summary gives the sense as far as the decode carries it. It is not a reading.

## Reading

None. The decode does not build sentences in Spanish, so I give no reading and no translation.

## The decode, row by row

The marks are explained in [README.md](README.md#how-to-read-the-decode). Each line gives the record, the image page and the row of my rows file.

- R9905, p. 1, C01: `… · · · ·-i-[unque] la su-[s]-{p} [en] si-r-{e} ·-a-s?-es ·-c-i-·-c-e-c-[io]-i-·-s?-·`

09Sources

  • Real Academia de la Historia, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) ff. 128–131, via DECODE R9877 and R9878 (images need a DECODE login).
  • Deciphered siblings used for the key: R9881 (ff. 138–140), R9883 and R9884 (ff. 168–173), R9890 (ff. 322–325), R9893 (ff. 483–485), R9897 (9/34 ff. 10–16), R9834 (9/29 ff. 33–39); deciphered in part here with the pass-16 key: R9873 (A-31 ff. 79–86) and R9898 (A-34 ff. 150–156, with six contemporary marginal decipherments); transcribed but not deciphered: R9895.
  • G. A. Bergenroth, Calendar of State Papers, Spain, vol. 2 (1866), nos. 630–654, on British History Online; the note on Goicoechea is under no. 637; nos. 636–638 are the triplicate, Goicoechea’s reading and the court abstract of R9873. Vol. iii.1 checked for R9898 (not calendared).
  • The contributed decode (section 07): DECODE records R9873 to R9881, R9883 to R9887, R9889 to R9901 and R9903 to R9905 (https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/ followed by the number; images need a DECODE login), Real Academia de la Historia, Colección Salazar y Castro A-31 (9/31) and A-34 (9/34). The clear texts behind its added values: Biblioteca Nacional de España, MSS/20214/52-1, and the clear leaves A-31 ff. 140, 172, 324, 482, 485 and 516. M. Héder and B. Megyesi, “The DECODE Database of Historical Ciphers and Keys: Version 2”, HistoCrypt 2022, doi:10.3384/ecp188397.

No page images are reproduced here: the scans are the Academy’s, served through DECODE to registered users.

Transcriptions, the key with its evidence, the token-cutting script and the template matcher are in the repository folder targets/sessa1524/.
